## Extrait du priuilege.

PAR priuilege expres du Roy noſtre Sire, daté du hui-
tieſme iour d’Aouſt l’an mil cinq cens cinquante ſix, qui
a eſté publié & enregiſtré en la cour de la Seneſchaucee
de Lyon, il a eſté permis à Macé Bonhomme, imprimeur
de Lyon, d’imprimer, & faire imprimer de telz characte-
res que bon luy ſemblera, mettre en vente, & debiter le
preſent liure, intitulé, *Des choſes merueilleuſes en nature.* Par
quoy ſont faites defenſes à tous libraires & imprimeurs
& autres, d’imprimer, ou faire imprimer le dit liure en
forme & maniere, quece ſoit, ou de ceux, qui auroyent e-
ſté contre faictz, ou imprimez ailleurs, n’en apporter, ny
expoſer en vente ès païs, & terres de ce Royaume, durant
le temps & termede dix ans, commençant du iour, que
l’impreſsiō du preſent liure ſera paracheuée, auec groſſes
peines contre ceux, qui contreuiendront directement
ou indirectement au dit priuilege. Par lequel eſt permis
d’inſerer, pour toutes defenſes & ſignifications, le ſom-
maire du dit priuilege, au cōmencemēt, ou ſur la fin du pre-
ſent liure: ainſi, que plus amplement eſt contenu au pri-
uilege ſuſdit.

La premiere impreſsion du preſent liure a eſté
acheuée le 18. Nouembre, 1557.

# Appendix 1: Questionnaire for expert survey
Introduction to the Survey

The primary focus of my research is "Understanding Complexity in Modern Software Architectures." My objective is to study the various perspectives on complexity in modern software development and identify the current state of software metrics used to manage this complexity.
Target Audience: Practitioners and researchers with hands-on experience in software development and/or research.

This survey consists of two parts. The first part focuses on understanding the concept and key indicators of software complexity, while the second part explores the use of software metrics in practice.
Completion time: 10-15 minutes.

PART 1: Understanding Software Complexity
Question 1: In your opinion, how would you define "software complexity"?
(Please share your personal or professional definition)
[Open text field]

Question 2: In your professional experience, which of the following aspects contribute most to the complexity of a software system? (Please select all that apply)
- Large number of lines of code
- Deep inheritance hierarchies
- High cyclomatic complexity (many decision points)
- Tight coupling between components
- Distributed systems (network latency, concurrency)
- Asynchronous execution
- Frequent changes and updates (velocity)
- Poor documentation
- Use of multiple technologies/languages
- External dependencies (libraries, APIs)
- Other (please specify)

Question 2.1: From the list above, which one do you consider the most significant factor?
[Dropdown/Radio buttons based on the list in Q2]

Question 3: How do you typically perceive complexity while working on a project?
- Difficult to understand the code logic
- Time-consuming to add new features
- High risk of introducing bugs when making changes
- Challenges in testing and debugging
- Difficulty in onboarding new team members
- Other (please specify)

PART 2: Software Metrics in Practice
Question 4: Do you currently use software metrics in your projects?
- Yes, systematically (automated tools, dashboards)
- Yes, occasionally (manual checks, ad-hoc analysis)
- No, but I have used them in the past
- No, I have never used them

[Logic: If Yes, go to Q5. If No, go to Q8]

Question 5: Which software metrics do you or your team use regularly? (Please select all that apply)
- Lines of Code (LOC)
- Cyclomatic Complexity
- Halstead Metrics
- Coupling (CBO, Efferent/Afferent Coupling)
- Cohesion (LCOM)
- Depth of Inheritance Tree (DIT)
- Code Coverage
- Code Smell Detection (Duplication, Long Methods, etc.)
- Deployment frequency/Mean Time to Repair (DORA metrics)
- Other (please specify)

Question 6: In your opinion, how effective are these metrics in managing or reducing software complexity?
- Highly effective
- Moderately effective
- Slightly effective
- Not effective at all
- It depends (please explain)

Question 7: How are the results of these metrics communicated or acted upon within your team?
- Integrated into the CI/CD pipeline (e.g., builds fail if thresholds are exceeded)
- Reviewed during code reviews
- Discussed in periodic team meetings
- Tracked on a project dashboard
- Mostly ignored
- Other (please specify)

Question 8: (For those who don't use metrics) What are the main reasons you don't use software metrics? (Please select all that apply)
- Lack of time
- Lack of knowledge about relevant metrics
- Tools are too expensive or difficult to set up
- Metrics do not accurately reflect actual complexity
- Management doesn't see the value
- Other (please specify)

Question 9: Do you believe modern architectures (e.g., Microservices, Serverless, AI-integrated systems) require new or different types of metrics compared to traditional monolithic systems?
- Yes (please explain why and what might be needed)
- No (please explain why)
- Not sure

PART 3: Background Information (Optional)
Question 10: What is your primary role?
- Developer / Software Engineer
- Architect
- DevOps Engineer
- Project Manager

# Appendix 3: Systematic mapping table
| Source | Authors | Title | Key Theme(s) | Findings |
|--------|---------|-------|--------------|----------|
| IEEE | S. Wagner | A Survey on Software Quality Models | Quality models, complexity | Standard models often lack context-specific complexity metrics. |
| ACM | N. Fenton | Software Metrics: A Rigorous and Practical Approach | Traditional metrics | Fundamental metrics like LOC and Cyclomatic Complexity are foundational but insufficient for distributed systems. |
| Springer | G. Bogner et al. | Assuring the Maintainability of Microservices | Microservices, maintainability | Complexity shifts from code to communication and infrastructure in microservices. |
| IEEE | C. Ebert et al. | Cyclomatic Complexity | Cyclomatic complexity | Remains relevant but needs integration with other metrics for a holistic view. |
| ScienceDirect | M. Rossi et al. | Complexity in Software Development | Structural/Cognitive complexity | Cognitive complexity is becoming more critical as systems become more abstract. |
| ACM | B. Meyer | Software Architecture: The Role of Complexity | Architectural complexity | Modularity and coupling are the primary drivers of architectural complexity. |
| IEEE | J. Lewis | Microservices: A Definition of This New Architectural Term | Microservices, architecture | Decentralization adds operational complexity. |
| Springer | T. Gschwind | Architectural Patterns for Distributed Systems | Distributed systems | Patterns can mitigate but also introduce new types of complexity (e.g., eventual consistency). |
| ACM | A. Hevery | The Clean Code Blog | Code quality, complexity | Focus on testability and clear separation of concerns to manage complexity. |
| IEEE | L. Bass et al. | Software Architecture in Practice | Quality attributes | Complexity affects all quality attributes, especially maintainability and scalability. |

In []:
# Appendix 4: Summary of selected relevant papers
- **S. Wagner, "A Survey on Software Quality Models":** This paper explores various software quality models and highlights that while many exist, they often fail to capture the specific nuances of complexity in modern, distributed environments.
- **N. Fenton, "Software Metrics: A Rigorous and Practical Approach":** A foundational text that categorizes metrics into product, process, and resource metrics. It provides a basis for understanding how traditional metrics can be adapted.
- **G. Bogner et al., "Assuring the Maintainability of Microservices":** This study focuses on how microservices change the landscape of software maintenance, shifting the burden from internal code structure to the interactions between services.
- **C. Ebert et al., "Cyclomatic Complexity":** This article discusses the enduring relevance of McCabe's cyclomatic complexity and its limitations in the context of modern programming paradigms.
- **M. Rossi et al., "Complexity in Software Development":** The authors argue that as systems grow more complex, the cognitive load on developers becomes the primary bottleneck, necessitating metrics that measure understandability.

In []:
# Appendix 5: Mapping of metrics to taxonomy
| Metric | Structural | Computational | Cognitive | Dynamic |
|--------|:----------:|:-------------:|:---------:|:-------:|

| Network Latency | | | | X |
| Resource Utilization | | | | X |

In []:
# Appendix 6: List of metrics
1.  **Lines of Code (LOC):** A simple measure of the size of a software system.
2.  **Cyclomatic Complexity (CC):** Measures the number of linearly independent paths through a program's source code.
3.  **Coupling Between Object Classes (CBO):** Counts the number of other classes to which a class is coupled.
4.  **Lack of Cohesion in Methods (LCOM):** Measures the degree to which methods in a class are related to each other.
5.  **Depth of Inheritance Tree (DIT):** Measures the maximum length from a class to the root of the tree.
6.  **Fan-in/Fan-out:** Measures the number of incoming and outgoing dependencies for a module.
7.  **Halstead Metrics:** Includes measures of program volume, effort, and difficulty based on operators and operands.
8.  **Cognitive Functional Size (CFS):** Measures complexity based on the cognitive load required to understand the logic.
9.  **Response Time:** Time taken for a system or service to respond to a request.
10. **Network Latency:** Time delay in communication between distributed components.
11. **Resource Utilization:** Measures the consumption of CPU, memory, and disk space.
12. **Deployment Frequency:** How often code is deployed to production.
13. **Mean Time to Repair (MTTR):** Average time taken to fix a failure in the system.
